## Not duplicates

lem-weil-signed-line-kflat-models vs lem-weil-signed-line-tensor-equivalence: the existing statement concerns signed line equivalences on a scheme's pro-etale site; the new lemma treats arbitrary sites and additionally states exactness, acyclic preservation, the total-complex rebracketing, flat-term K-flat stability and successive arbitrary coefficient models.
lem-weil-signed-line-kflat-models vs lem-sf-integral-line-tensor-comparison-representative: that statement fixes a completed finite-field coefficient sheaf and a quotient reduction; the new lemma has arbitrary site and ring tower, signed powers and their exact inverse tensor equivalences and common successive models.
lem-weil-signed-line-kflat-models vs lem-pur-kflat-total-tensor-model: that theorem assumes both complexes already K-flat and flat-term; the new statement constructs the signed degree-zero lines, proves their exact inverse action and makes them suitable factors, and handles the arbitrary coefficient tower. It is not an application with its missing line hypotheses silently assumed.
lem-weil-signed-line-kflat-models vs lem-pur-signed-line-derived-base-change: the existing lemma concerns a geometric inverse-image functor with structural ring isomorphism and normalized signed-power maps; the new lemma concerns exact tensor action and K-flat common models under arbitrary, possibly nonflat ring maps.
lem-weil-ordinary-line-tower-square vs lem-sfb-flat-scalar-tower-coherence: the existing derived part assumes all scalar functors exact; the new lemma permits arbitrary sheaf-ring maps and derives its natural line/tower square using K-flat models. It also identifies the line-crossing paths explicitly.
lem-weil-ordinary-line-tower-square vs lem-sp-scalar-extension-dual-tower-core: the existing statement is underived dual/tensor coherence, explicitly with no power tower squares; the new statement descends cochain maps to all unbounded derived objects and proves the line/tower square there.
lem-weil-ordinary-line-tower-square vs lem-weil-integral-twist-derived-scalar: the latter gives a single completed coefficient twist comparison, with no tower equation or general sheaf-ring hypothesis. The new lemma is solely ordinary and allows arbitrary ring maps and supplied lines.
lem-weil-ordinary-line-tower-square vs lem-pur-completed-line-tensor-coherence: the latter concerns finite extensions of Q_l and completed pro-etale coefficient sheaves; the new lemma works for arbitrary rings and sites without completion, is independent of quotient-tower comparisons, and asserts natural descent of its explicit chain associator. A result restricted to finite l-adic coefficient towers cannot specialize to this general statement.
lem-weil-natural-comparison-tower-transport vs lem-pur-completed-line-tensor-coherence: the new lemma is a categorical transport theorem for arbitrary categories and supplied comparison functors; it does not assert that any coefficient line, completion comparison or tensor associator exists. The existing theorem's coefficient hypotheses and constructions cannot supply that general statement.
lem-weil-natural-comparison-tower-transport vs lem-fix-scalar-completion-associator: the existing statement constructs and normalizes a particular strict-tower coefficient associator; the new one assumes a general ordinary associator and arbitrary natural chi, and transports a supplied intertwining square and units.
lem-weil-natural-comparison-tower-transport vs lem-amb-natural-transformation-whisker-exchange: a single two-transformation interchange equation is a tool for, not the statement of, the triangular transport/cancellation theorem with specified associators, identity normalization and scalar localization.
lem-weil-natural-comparison-tower-transport vs lem-pur-semilinear-scalar-localization: the latter supplies the localization of natural transformations and their equations, not the pre-localization tower-transport and unit equations that are the main new argument. Its general theorem may be cited by the future worker for the final localization clause.
lem-weil-natural-comparison-tower-transport vs lem-derived-exact-equivalence-squares: that lemma constructs comparisons of derived adjoints through equivalences of abelian categories; no such categories, adjunctions or resolutions are hypotheses of the new categorical cancellation theorem.

lem-weil-ordinary-line-tower-square vs lem-pur-signed-line-scalar-tensor-tower: the existing statement gives underived signed-power, evaluation and scalar/tensor compatibility; the new node asserts a natural square on all unbounded derived complexes, via explicit flat-term K-flat chain maps and descent, for arbitrary ring maps.

## Full preservation and allocation of the returned argument

The following nine paragraphs are copied verbatim from the returned/live proof. They are retained as proof material for future workers, not as proof steps of the new unsplit nodes. Allocation: old step 1 is the short domain/notation assembly and the input bookkeeping for all children; steps 2-3 go to signed-line-kflat-models; steps 4-5 go to ordinary-line-tower-square; steps 6-7 go to natural-comparison-tower-transport; step 8's signed line multiplication goes to signed-line-kflat-models, ordinary chain unit goes to ordinary-line-tower-square, and completed unit transport goes to natural-comparison-tower-transport; step 9's rational naturality/conjugation argument goes to natural-comparison-tower-transport, and its named-map identification is supplied in assembly step 5 by the existing explicit completed-line normalization. Thus no old argument is silently deleted or replaced by a compressed assertion.

### Original step 1 — domain assembly and inputs of all three children

Put $L_H=\mathcal O_H(m)$, $W_H(K)=K(m)$, $T_{J/H}(K)=K\otimes^L_{R_H}R_J$, and $B_{J/H}=\operatorname{Ext}_{J/H}$. Write $\theta_{J/H}:L_H\otimes_{R_H}R_J\xrightarrow{\sim}L_J$ for [lem-weil-integral-twist-base-change]. Its tower clause gives, for every local section $l$ of $L_E$,
$$\theta_{G/F}(\theta_{F/E}(l\otimes1)\otimes1)=\theta_{G/E}(l\otimes1).$$
This holds for every integer $m$, including dual twists. By [lem-weil-integral-twist-derived-scalar], the ordinary comparison $\alpha_{J/H,K}:T_{J/H}W_HK\to W_JT_{J/H}K$ has the displayed chain formula in that node and its conjugate is $\beta_{J/H,K}$. In particular that node gives the comparisons in the completed integral categories, so $(B_{J/H}K)(m)\in D^b_c(S,\mathcal O_J)$ for $K\in D^b_c(S,\mathcal O_H)$. For $C(m)$ itself apply its case $F=E$: $(B_{E/E}C)(m)\in D^b_c(S,\mathcal O_E)$, and $C\cong B_{E/E}C$ by $\chi_{E/E,C}$ after the multiplication isomorphism; so $C(m)$ lies in this full subcategory, defined by isomorphism-invariant conditions, and is derived complete. The natural isomorphisms $\chi_{J/H,K}:T_{J/H}K\to B_{J/H}K$ are those of [lem-weil-finite-free-completed-scalar-comparison]. Derived completeness of $C$ follows from [def-weil-derived-complete-integral-detailed]; [lem-weil-integral-scalar-extension-constructible] keeps the successive completed extensions in the integral categories, so every displayed comparison is applicable.

### Original step 2 — lem-weil-signed-line-kflat-models

The rank-one first twist in clause 3 of [lem-pur-twist-sheaf-locally-free] trivializes on a pro-etale cover. Tensor restriction [lem-sfb-localized-site-tensor-restriction], the tensor-power definition [def-coefficient-twists-detailed], and the localized definition of sheaf Hom [def-pur-sheaf-hom] show that $L_H$ and its dual are free of rank one on that same cover for all signs of $m$. The evaluation maps $L_H\otimes L_H^\vee\to R_H$ and $L_H^\vee\otimes L_H\to R_H$ are locally multiplication, hence isomorphisms: their unique local inverses agree and glue. By [lem-pc-sheaf-modules-tensor-closed], tensor with these two lines gives inverse additive equivalences. These are exact: an inverse equivalence identifies each kernel or cokernel universal problem with the original one. Thus $L_H$ is flat in the sense of [def-pc-flat-sheaf-modules], and tensor with it preserves acyclic complexes by [lem-weil-pbc-exact-functor-complex-cohomology].

### Original step 3 — lem-weil-signed-line-kflat-models

Choose $P\to C$ K-flat with flat terms by [lem-pc-kflat-resolution]. Then $P\otimes_{R_E}L_E$ is also K-flat with flat terms. Indeed its terms are flat because their tensor functors are composites of exact tensor functors; for any acyclic $Q$, associativity identifies $Q\otimes(P\otimes L_E)$ with $(Q\otimes P)\otimes L_E$, acyclic by [def-pc-kflat-complex] and step 2. In total tensor [def-pc-tensor-complex-sheaves], the line is concentrated in degree zero. Rebracketing sums is valid because the tensor-Hom adjunction of [lem-pc-sheaf-modules-tensor-closed] implies preservation of direct sums (both sides represent the product of the Hom sets of the summands). There are no additional differential signs. Apply [lem-pur-kflat-change-of-rings] to $R_E\to R_F$ and then $R_F\to R_G$, both for $P$ and for $P\otimes L_E$. Thus all iterated scalar extensions in the square are computed on these same complexes by [lem-pur-derived-change-of-rings]. Step 1's chain comparison computes $\alpha$ on them.

### Original step 4 — lem-weil-ordinary-line-tower-square

For $K=C$ or $C(m)$, the ordinary associator $\mu_K:T_{G/F}T_{F/E}K\to T_{G/E}K$ is represented on the models of step 3 by
$$((x\otimes b)\otimes c)\longmapsto x\otimes\iota_{FG}(b)c,$$
with inverse $x\otimes c\mapsto(x\otimes1)\otimes c$, where $\iota_{FG}:R_F\to R_G$ is the coefficient inclusion. These maps are balanced; their less immediate composite is the identity because $(x\otimes b)\otimes c=(x\otimes1)\otimes\iota_{FG}(b)c$. The bilinear universal property [lem-pc-sites-sheaf-tensor-bilinear-universal] and its extension through sheafification [lem-pc-sites-module-sheafification-bilinear-extension] make the formulas sheaf morphisms and allow equality to be checked on these inputs. Since only $x$ has nonzero degree, they are inverse chain maps. By [lem-pur-derived-change-of-rings] they compute the required ordinary derived associativity map. The formulas commute with every chain map of $P$. In a sufficiently large module universe the small pro-etale site meets the size hypothesis of [lem-weil-pbc-site-derived-tensor-descent]; its equivalence from the localization of K-flat flat-term models to the derived category extends this naturality to every derived morphism, since naturality for a quasi-isomorphism also gives naturality for its inverse. Thus $\mu$ is natural and independent of the model, not merely an objectwise isomorphism.

Cited nodes: lem-pc-sites-sheaf-tensor-bilinear-universal, lem-pc-sites-module-sheafification-bilinear-extension, lem-pur-derived-change-of-rings, lem-weil-pbc-site-derived-tensor-descent.

Source: literature/stacks-project/sites-modules.tex; Tag 03EK, section-tensor-product and bilinear universal property after Tag 0GMW; mixed coefficient associator written explicitly.

### Original step 5 — lem-weil-ordinary-line-tower-square

Start with $u=(((x\otimes l)\otimes b)\otimes c)$ in the common model $((P\otimes_{R_E}L_E)\otimes_{R_E}R_F)\otimes_{R_F}R_G$. By the formula for $\alpha$ in [lem-weil-integral-twist-derived-scalar], the path that first commutes each extension with twisting gives successively
$$((x\otimes b)\otimes\theta_{F/E}(l\otimes1))\otimes c,$$
$$((x\otimes b)\otimes c)\otimes\theta_{G/F}(\theta_{F/E}(l\otimes1)\otimes1),$$
and finally
$$(x\otimes\iota_{FG}(b)c)\otimes\theta_{G/F}(\theta_{F/E}(l\otimes1)\otimes1).$$
The other path applies $\mu_{C(m)}$ and then $\alpha_{G/E,C}$, giving
$$(x\otimes\iota_{FG}(b)c)\otimes\theta_{G/E}(l\otimes1).$$
The tower clause of [lem-weil-integral-twist-base-change] equates these outputs. Repeated use of the bilinear/sheafification universal properties cited in step 4 makes this equality an equality of chain maps, without assuming that global sections are sums of global pure tensors. Therefore, after passage to the derived category,
$$W_G(\mu_C)\,\alpha_{G/F,T_{F/E}C}\,T_{G/F}(\alpha_{F/E,C})=\alpha_{G/E,C}\,\mu_{W_EC}.$$
This is the asserted tensor formula, with $bc$ meaning $\iota_{FG}(b)c$ and $l\otimes1$ interpreted by $\theta_{G/E}$.

### Original step 6 — lem-weil-natural-comparison-tower-transport

For compact notation put $T_1=T_{F/E}$, $T_2=T_{G/F}$, $T_3=T_{G/E}$ and similarly $B_i,\chi_i,\alpha_i,\beta_i$; put $W_0=W_E$, $W_1=W_F$, $W_2=W_G$. Composition in the next equations is read right to left. Define
$$d_K=\chi_{2,B_1K}\,T_2(\chi_{1,K}):T_2T_1K\xrightarrow{\sim}B_2B_1K.$$
By naturality of the specified $\chi$ in [lem-weil-finite-free-completed-scalar-comparison], this also equals $B_2(\chi_{1,K})\,\chi_{2,T_1K}$. The stipulated transport defining $a$ is exactly
$$a_K=\chi_{3,K}\,\mu_K\,d_K^{-1}.$$
The conjugation defining the specified twist comparison in [lem-weil-integral-twist-derived-scalar] is exactly
$$\beta_{i,K}=W_j(\chi_{i,K})\,\alpha_{i,K}\,\chi_{i,W_hK}^{-1}$$
for $i=(j/h)$. These equations use the fixed natural comparisons, rather than any chosen isomorphisms between complete objects.

### Original step 7 — lem-weil-natural-comparison-tower-transport

Here is the cancellation in the left-hand path. Naturality of $\chi_2$ for $\beta_{1,C}$ gives
$$B_2(\beta_{1,C})\,\chi_{2,B_1W_0C}=\chi_{2,W_1B_1C}\,T_2(\beta_{1,C}).$$
Naturality of $\alpha_2$ for $\chi_{1,C}$ gives
$$\alpha_{2,B_1C}\,T_2W_1(\chi_{1,C})=W_2T_2(\chi_{1,C})\,\alpha_{2,T_1C}.$$
The first is supplied by [lem-weil-finite-free-completed-scalar-comparison]. The second follows from [lem-weil-integral-twist-derived-scalar]: its $\beta$ is natural, and conjugating by natural $\chi$ makes $\alpha$ natural as well. Expanding $d_{W_0C}$ and both $\beta$ maps in step 6 and using the first equation cancels $\chi_{2,W_1B_1C}^{-1}\chi_{2,W_1B_1C}$ and $T_2(\chi_{1,W_0C}^{-1}\chi_{1,W_0C})$. The second equation then gives
$$\beta_{2,B_1C}\,B_2(\beta_{1,C})\,d_{W_0C}=W_2(d_C)\,\alpha_{2,T_1C}\,T_2(\alpha_{1,C}).$$
Multiplying by $W_2(a_C)$ cancels $W_2(d_C^{-1}d_C)$, leaving $W_2(\chi_{3,C})$ followed by the left side of the ordinary equality in step 5. Expanding the right-hand path gives
$$\beta_{3,C}\,a_{W_0C}\,d_{W_0C}=W_2(\chi_{3,C})\,\alpha_{3,C}\,\mu_{W_0C}.$$
Step 5 equates these expressions. Cancel the isomorphism $d_{W_0C}$ to obtain precisely the required completed coherence identity. Since the integral categories are full [def-weil-derived-complete-integral-detailed], the equality holds there as well.

### Original step 8 — all three children, divided into signed-line, ordinary and transported units as above

For the identity coefficient extension $H=J$, the map $\theta_{H/H}$ of [lem-weil-integral-twist-base-change] is the tensor unit. Indeed, on the local generator $e^m$ from step 2 its associativity construction sends $e^m\otimes b$ to $be^m$ for $m\geq0$; for $m<0$ its dual-base-change construction has the same formula, as evaluation on $e^{-m}$ shows. Let $u_K:K\otimes^L_{R_H}R_H\to K$ be multiplication. The ordinary twist comparison satisfies
$$W_H(u_K)\,\alpha_{H/H,K}=u_{W_HK}:$$
on $(x\otimes l)\otimes b$ both sides give $bx\otimes l=x\otimes bl$. The ordinary associator of step 4 with $F=E$ gives $x\otimes bc=bx\otimes c$; with $G=F$ it gives the target multiplication unit. By [lem-weil-finite-free-completed-scalar-comparison], $\chi_{H/H,K}$ is the derived-completeness unit after the multiplication identification. Hence the completed identity comparison is $u_K\chi_{H/H,K}^{-1}$, and substituting it into the displayed equality proves the identity-extension assertion for $\beta$ and $a$. All calculations include $m=0$, negative $m$, $C=0$ and the empty site; no faithful-fibre argument is used.

### Original step 9 — lem-weil-natural-comparison-tower-transport; named normalization in parent step 5

By [def-weil-rationalization-karoubi], rationalization retains the same integral objects and tensors Hom groups with the coefficient field. It therefore sends the isomorphism $a_C$ and its inverse to inverse morphisms, preserving all equations above. The resulting map
$$(C\otimes_EF)\otimes_FG\longrightarrow C\otimes_EG$$
is natural even for rational morphisms: for $f\otimes t$, $t\in E$, tensor the integral naturality equation by $t$, and extend additively to finite sums. It is the scalar-extension composition isomorphism named in [lem-weil-scalar-extension-composition]: its source constructs that canonical map by integral tensor associativity followed by rationalization. More explicitly, representing both sides through $\chi$ gives the multiplication formula $((x\otimes b)\otimes c)\mapsto x\otimes\iota_{FG}(b)c$ from step 4, so the integral map before rationalization is exactly $\chi_{3,C}\mu_Cd_C^{-1}$, not an arbitrary natural isomorphism. This identifies the specified maps and proves the last clause.
